- **Kashmir** (Pass 1, c. 032, lists it outside Pakistan) — "والی کشمیر", Ayaz's father's realm (p. 410); its hills, "دره" and "لوه کوت" (pp. 420, 416, 414). Sacred waters: "نیله ناگ", for divination with walnuts ("جوز"); the book "نیل متهه" sets out its temples; a drowned city visited by a Brahman in "بدشاه[OCR?]"'s time; twin hot and cold springs where bones are burned; a mountain pool for bones and ashes; a spring at "موضع باروا[OCR?]" curing lepers ("مبروصان") at Sunday dawn; the lake "اولر[OCR?]", 28 kos round, fed by the "بهت" (p. 507); the hot ground of "سوهیم[OCR?]", gold-washing at "کمراج[OCR?]" and Gilgit (p. 507); the saffron spring at "موضع زیبون[OCR?]", the springs "شکرناگ[OCR?]" and "کوتهار[OCR?]", the tank "دیوسر[OCR?]", "موضع پیتلهام[OCR?]" (p. 508). "صوبه دل پذیر کشمیر" (p. 509): Srinagar, "چهار فرسنگ آبادی", rivers "بهت و مار[OCR?] و کچهه[OCR?]"; shawls sent "در هفت کشور", "درمه(۱)[OCR?]" (editor's variant "دهیه[OCR?]") and pattu; wooden houses of four storeys with tulips on the roofs; the lake "دل[OCR?]"; boats, many Brahmans, Sanskrit books on "توز" bark; Hindus hold the whole country a place of worship; springs at "سنده براری[OCR?]" and "گوگرناگ[OCR?]"; breaks off at "در موضع مین[OCR?] پوریده[OCR?] دوازده هزار". Contents headings at printed pages ۳۹۵, ۴۱۰, ۴۱۲ (p. 591) and ۴۶۷ (p. 590); sources "نسخه راج ترنگینی" and a "تاریخ کشمیری" of "چهار هزار … سال", which "مولانائے شاه محمد شاه ابادی از لغت کشمیری بفارسی دراورده" (pp. 582–581); Sharada not named.
- **Ghazni and the north-west beyond the subas** (Pass 1, c. 032: outside Pakistan) — Ghazni, Ghur, Firuzkuh, Badakhshan and "کرمان" (Yildiz's refuge) (pp. 400–410); Ghazni, "که ان را زابل گویند", capital of Sabuktigin, Mahmud and Shihab al-Din Ghori, "خوابگاه حکیم سنائی و بسیاری اولیاست", called "دروازه ایران" (p. 505); Badakhshan ۳۹۲ and Orissa and Kandahar ۴۱۳ among the contents headings (p. 591).
- **Other places outside Pakistan in the sultanate chapters (Pass 1's lists)** — Delhi, Firozabad, Daulatabad/Devgir, the Doab, Baran, Qannauj, "مونهه[OCR?]", Tirhut, Bengal (pp. 346–347); Gujarat, Bharuch, "سرنال[OCR?]" (p. 345); Hansi, "حصار فیروزه", Sirhind and Samana, Firozpur "بر لب دریاے ستلج و بیاه که یک جا بیرود[OCR?]", the Jumna canals, Sirmur (pp. 343–344, 337–338); Jalandhar, Kotla Mewat, Mirath, Jalesar ("محمد آباد"), Kampil, Sunam (pp. 335–336). Kara ("کوره", editor's variant "کره"), Manikpur, Devgadh ("دیوگده"), Ranthambhor, Chitor, Chanderi, Malwa, Badaun, Gujarat, "سنگلدیب[OCR?]" (pp. 362–370). Delhi, Kilokhri, the Jamuna, Rohtak, Samana, Lakhnauti/Bengal, the Sarju, Kara, Ranthambhor[OCR?] (chunk 030). Delhi, Meerut, Kol, Gwalior, Budaun, "برن[OCR?]", Kanauj, Kuhram, Sirhind, Narain/Talawari, Sarsuti[OCR?], Hansi, Ajmer, the "کوه سوالک", Malwa, Awadh and Sirmur (pp. 397–404). Contents headings: Patna and Bengal ۳۷۹, "اطراف دکن" ۴۲۳, Asir and Ahmadnagar ۴۲۷, Golconda ۴۳۱ (p. 591).

## Citable passages

*The original script is given exactly as Pass 1 quoted it, with every flag. The English is a plain gloss (Pass 1's). Page references are PDF pages. Archive ids are given only where Pass 1 mapped the passage, and the Doubts section has their caveats.*

1. **Aurangzeb at the tomb of Bahauddin Zakariya, Multan** (`shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya`).
   > "نهم محرم الحرام بزیارت روضه منوره فیض امین[OCR?] عارف صمدانی قطب ربانی شیخ بهاء الدین ذکریا ملتانی قدس الله سره العزیز تشریف برده"
   > "استمداد همت و استفاضه انوار توجه نمودند و بوقت امدن و رفتن از روشنائی جیب مراد مساکین برآوردند[OCR?]"

   "On 9 Muharram he went to visit the illumined tomb of … Shaikh Bahauddin Zakariya Multani. … He sought the help of [the saint's] spiritual power and the outpouring of his lights, and as he came and went [apparently] gave to the poor." The sense of "از روشنائی" is not settled (p. 65).

2. **The death of Bahauddin Zakariya** (`shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya`).
   > "از چهار کنج خانه اواز بلند شد که دوست بادوست پیوست"

   "From the four corners of the house a loud voice arose: the friend has joined the Friend." (p. 528)

3. **Shaikh Rukn al-Din saves Multan** (`shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam`; *basis: a mapping by name only, "شیخ رکن الدین"*).
   > "سلطان خواست که اهل ملتان را قتل عام نماید به شفاعت شیخ الاسلام شیخ رکن الدین قدس الله سره نجات داده"

   "The sultan meant to massacre the people of Multan; at the intercession of Shaikh al-Islam Shaikh Rukn al-Din he spared them." (p. 347)

4. **A prince who fell out with a Multan saint (the author's voice).**
   > "مقرر است که هر کس که با درویشان خدا شناس در افتد زود که بنیاد زندگی و دولت او بر افتد"

   "It is established that whoever falls out with God-knowing dervishes soon sees the foundation of his life and fortune fall." The author says this of Prince Muhammad Sultan and Shaikh Sadr al-Din, son of Bahauddin Zakariya (p. 385).

5. **Sultan Sarwar's custodians.**
   > "اولاد انها را که مجاورت مزار دارند تا حال کاه و کلنگ نامند"

   "Their descendants, who hold the custodianship of the tomb, are still called Kah-o-Kulang." (p. 527)

6. **Makhdum Jahaniyan's dervishes (Uch).**
   > "درویشان ملنگ و دوداے سراپا برهنه از مریدان ان جناب درهرجانب هستند و ذکر جلی مینمایند"

   "Malang and Dodai dervishes, wholly naked, his disciples, are found everywhere and perform the loud dhikr." (p. 527)

7. **Shaikh Farid Ganj-i Shakar (the book's place: "پتن عرف اجودهن").**
   > "چون از برکات نظر کیمیا اثر ایشان تودهاے خاک شکر گردید بگنج شکر مشهور شدند"

   "Because heaps of earth turned to sugar by the blessing of his alchemical gaze, he became known as Ganj-i Shakar." (p. 526)

8. **Hujwiri at Lahore.**
   > "از غزنین همراه سلطان محمود آمده درلاهور رخت هستی بربست و سلطان فتح لاهور را از برکات قدوم ایشان میدانست"

   "He came from Ghazni with Sultan Mahmud and died in Lahore; the Sultan held the conquest of Lahore to be the blessing of his arrival." (p. 523)

9. **Shah Daula (Gujrat).**
   > "درایام حیات ان ولایت پناه عالم عالم خلایق از اطراف گیتی بزیارت می آمد"

   "In the lifetime of that refuge of sainthood, throngs upon throngs came from every quarter of the world for ziyarat." (p. 515)

10. **Baba Nanak's followers (suba Lahore).**
    > "اگر شخصی نیم شبی وارد شود و اسم بابا نانک درمیان ارد ... او را برادر و دوست انگاشته"

    "If someone arrives at midnight and speaks the name of Baba Nanak … they treat him as brother and friend." (p. 519)

11. **The dervishes' dream in the Thatta country.**
    > "بعضے درویشان صاحب حال در خواب دیدند که سکه و خطبه بنام نامی حضرت خدیو جهان درین دیار می شود"

    "Certain dervishes of spiritual state saw in dreams that coin and khutba would be struck in the emperor's name in this land." (p. 174)

12. **The coming of Islam to Multan.**
    > "در ولایت ملتان آغاز ظهور اسلام در سنه نود و نه هجری از محمد قاسم در زمان حجاج بن یوسف است"

    "In the country of Multan, Islam first appeared in the year ninety-nine Hijri, through Muhammad Qasim, in the time of Hajjaj ibn Yusuf." (p. 298)

13. **One lamp for Kaaba and idol-house (the author's voice).**
    > "در حیرتم که دشمنی کفر و دین چراست   از یک چراغ کعبه و بتخانه روشن است"
    > "این سجایای عامیان هر گروه است که پی بمطلب اصلی نبرده تعصب را عبادت می پندارند"

    "I wonder why unbelief and faith are at enmity: the Kaaba and the idol-house are lit from one lamp." "These are the traits of the common people of every group, who, missing the real aim, take bigotry for worship." (p. 586)

14. **The author on himself.**
    > "از عنفوان ظهور صبح شعور بملازمت ناظمان امور مملکت و مال … پیشه خطوط نویسی که عبارت از منشیگری بوده باشد بسر برده"

    "From the first dawn of awareness he spent [his life] in the service of the administrators of state and revenue, in the profession of letter-writing, that is, the munshi's craft." (p. 583)

15. **The date of composition.**
    > "در سنه چهلم عالم گیری مطابق یک هزار و یکصد و هفتم هجری و هزار و هفتصد و پنجاه و سه سنه بکرماجیت"

    "In the fortieth year of Alamgir, corresponding to 1107 Hijri and 1753 of the Bikramajit era." The same sentence also gives a Salbahan and a Kaliyuga year; none is converted (p. 581).

## Doubts and review points

*Everything below is what Pass 1 recorded under Doubts, merged across the 48 chunks. Chunks are named "c. NNN"; pages are PDF pages (this book cites no folios). Index, errata and contents numbers are the 1918 edition's printed pages, quoted as text. Mappings are frozen at Pass 1: the caveats and declines below are reported, not reversed, and no date is converted or reconciled. Every `[OCR?]` and `[illegible]` flag stands on the word Pass 1 put it on.*

### Mapping inconsistencies and formerly held identities for Rauf

*Where Pass 1 treated the same kind of name or place differently in different chunks, each chunk's treatment is reproduced as it stands. Choosing one treatment is Rauf's decision.*

**Formerly held identities (lifted 10 Oct 2026; attached as this book's claim, never resolved here)**

- **langer-makhdoom.** c. 041 maps the book's "شاه برهان" to the row's "Makhdoom Burhan-ud-din" by name; the city is printed "چندتوت[OCR?]", and the reading "چنیوت" (Chiniot) is Pass 1's own, offered only because of the flag; the facts about the city (ancient, two hillocks) are place-level. c. 023 maps the row place-level only, through the toponym "chiniot", whose single row it is: the row's location reads "Langer Makhdoom, Chiniot", the book's "قصبه چنیوت" is a town, and no saint is named there. Not mapped: the index's "شاہ برہان" ("۷۸"), a candidate on the name only — "چنیوٹ" also carries "۷۸", a coincidence of locators, not an identification the index makes (c. 003); the index's Chiniot, left as a single-row toponym without ids (c. 003); "برہان الدین غریب - ۳۴ [OCR?]" (p. 42), whose "غریب" the row does not carry (c. 004); "مخدوم عالم پناه" (p. 320), the book's epithet for the Samana dervish, not a name (c. 025).
- **The Uch Jalal / Jahaniyan split.** c. 042 found a way to choose for the Uch tomb: "خوابگاه شیخ جلال پور سید محمود بن سید جلال بخاری مشهور بمخدوم جهانیانست" — the man buried at Uch, Shaikh Jalal son of Sayyid Mahmud, is "known as Makhdum Jahaniyan" — so the tomb facts go to **shrine-of-makhdoom-jahaniyan-jahangasht** alone. His grandfather "سید جلال بخاری" is mapped by bare name to **shrine-of-jalaluddin-surkh-posh-bukhari-jalaluddin-bukhari**; the book does not call him Surkh-Posh, does not place his tomb at Uch and gives no date. Not resolved. The genealogy is read "Shaikh Jalal, son (پور) of Sayyid Mahmud, son (بن) of Sayyid Jalal Bukhari"; Jahaniyan's death year "هفتصد و هشتاد و پنج" has no era word, unlike his birth "هفتصد و هفت هجری" (c. 042). Not mapped elsewhere: the index's "مخدوم جہانیان" — ۶۲[OCR?] (p. 20), which names no place and has a flagged locator; a separate entry "شیخ جلال" carries the same printed ۶۲ (p. 26) and "ملتان" also lists ۶۲ (p. 20), only an index coincidence — check printed page ۶۲ in the body (c. 002); the index's "جلال بخاری" ("۶۲"), no way to choose (c. 003); "شاه عالم بخاری" and "قطب عالم" (p. 533), placed by the book at Ahmadabad and Batwa, not Uch (c. 043). Both rows otherwise ride the Uch place-level lines only (c. 004, 022, 026, 032), where neither saint is named.
- **shrine-of-shah-shams-ud-din-sabzwari ("Shams", Multan).** c. 042 maps the book's "شمس الدین تبریزی", in the Multan burial list, to this (Sabzwari) row as the brief directs; the nisbas differ and the book gives nothing else. c. 045 does NOT map "شمس الدین تبریزی" (p. 555), because the book places him in Rum with Maulana Rumi, not at Multan — left for Rauf. The index's "شمس الدین تبریزی" — ۳۴, ۶۱ (p. 27) names no Multan and is not mapped; printed ۶۱ also appears under "ملتان", "فریدالدین گنجشکر", "شہاب الدین سہروردی", "شیخ عراقی", "صدرالدین" and "کمال الدین علی شاہ", which looks like a cluster of saints on one printed page — an inference about the index layout only (c. 002). Everywhere else the row rides the Multan place-level line only and nothing raises its question (c. 002, 006, 007, 011, 018, 021, 022, 025, 027, 028, 029, 030, 033).
- **Held rows on the Lahore line.** shrine-of-baba-shah-chiragh and shrine-of-shah-inayat-qadiri are attached only place-level, wherever the 35 Lahore ids are attached; no name in any chunk raises their questions (c. 002, 006, 007, 009, 010, 011, 012, 013, 014, 016, 020, 021, 022, 024, 025, 026, 027, 030, 031, 032, 033, 042). "عبدالرزاق" (p. 51), set with a eunuch to guard the Bhakkar fort, is a bare common name for a garrison officer and is NOT mapped to shrine-of-baba-shah-chiragh (Syed Abdul Razzaq) (c. 004). "عنایت" is ordinary vocabulary (pp. 262, 272, c. 021; pp. 311, 314, c. 025).

**1. Bare index headwords (c. 001-004).** Index material is editor's matter: it carries locators (printed pages of the 1918 edition, quoted as text, never converted), not facts.
- c. 001 mapped 20 ids from three headwords, none carrying a fact: Guru Nanak's 18 rows person-level on "نانک بابا", which names no site; **shrine-of-shah-yusaf-gardez** on "یوسف گردیزی" — the index does not say "Shah" or "Multan"; **data-darbar** on "میرعلی حجویری", printed with ح, with no "Ganj Bakhsh" and no Lahore — an identification to verify at locator ۶۵.
- c. 002 mapped place-level by ruling (a), from bare headwords: Lahore (35 rows), Multan (8 rows), and Shorkot (1 row: "شورکوت" → **garh-maharaja-shorkot** through toponym_map's "shorkot", the row located "Garh Maharaja (Shorkot)"; the index does not say its Shorkot is that place). It mapped Fariduddin Ganjshakar and Qutbuddin Aibak person-level on bare index names: each headword names the row's principal figure, but the index says nothing about a tomb.
- c. 003 mapped two lines on bare headwords — "دولا شاہ" (the brief's "شاه دوله" in inverted order) and "سلطان سرور" (one of the brief's forms for Sakhi Sarwar; its last locator "۷۳" is flagged) — "ids only where a headword names a row's saint or site"; the identification rests on the name alone. Following that instruction it LEFT WITHOUT IDS the same kind of toponym headword that c. 002 and c. 004 mapped: Sehwan (`lal-shahbaz-qalandar`, `shrine-of-lakhi-shah-saddar`), Sialkot (5 rows), Sukkur (5), Rawalpindi (6), Jhelum (2); and the single-row toponyms Khushab → `amb-temples-amb-sharif`, Hasan Abdal → `gurdwara-panja-sahib`, Chiniot → `langer-makhdoom`, Jhang → `garh-maharaja-shorkot`. "The place name alone does not name the site, so these attachments are left to the consolidator, place-level."
- c. 004 mapped place-level from bare index headwords with no text: Uch (5), Peshawar (10; the headword is flagged), Ichhra (2; as in c. 006, shrine-of-baba-shah-kamal is only "near Ichhra" in the index), Amarkot (2; "امرکوٹ" matched to both toponym_map entries, "umerkot" → umarkot-amarkot-shiv-mandir and "umarkot" → shrine-of-makhdoom-abdul-rahim-girhori, "near Pithoro, Umarkot"), Eminabad (3; reading "امن آباد" as Eminabad is a transliteration judgement), Badin (1; "بدین" taken as Badin). Guru Nanak (18) person-level on "بابا نانک". **shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya** on a bare headword whose locator "۵۲۴ [OCR?]" is flagged. The Uch line carries the held Jalaluddin / Jahaniyan pair by place only.

**2. Campaign, office and transit mentions heading place-level groups.** Pass 1's own words for the basis, chunk by chunk (tag `*basis: passing / campaign / office mention*`):
- Multan (8): a bare departure point, "ملتان[OCR?]" flagged, "one short bullet at most, so the consolidator may drop it" (c. 005); campaign material, but treasury, governor, chief zamindar, camp (c. 006); only that Multan was in Dara's iqta, with Bhakkar and Thatta (c. 007); a bare transit, Ali Quli meeting the Khankhanan (p. 142) (c. 011); governor Said Khan, a battle site "تابع ملتان", Baluchis "انطرف ملتان" — "a consolidator may judge it too thin" (c. 018); a single retrospective clause about a fief grant (p. 241) (c. 019); a bare "بسمت ملتان" / "تا ملتان" (p. 269) (c. 021); passing, with the one statement "اوچ تابع ملتان" (c. 022); the bare "ملتان" of Kamran's jagir (p. 298) and the flagged "از توابع ملتان[OCR?]" (p. 305), "both are thin" (c. 024); governor "ملک مروان" and the Lodi residence (p. 322), and the bare "بجانب ملتان راهی شده بود" (p. 318) (c. 025); revolt, threatened massacre, letters (c. 027); one campaign mention, "بهرام حاکم ملتان" (p. 354), "نیز[OCR?]" flagged (c. 028); the princes' flight, the siege, the counsel to fortify (c. 029); where the summoned man came from and Chhajju's confinement (c. 030); passing at p. 411 and the second campaign p. 405 (c. 032); passing "از راه ملتان" (p. 421) and "براه ریگستان متوجه ملتان" (p. 412) (c. 033); only directional, as a boundary for Afghan lands and the Nikshab tuman (pp. 502, 503), "mapped only because ruling (a) requires it" (c. 040); one bare toponym in Qutbuddin Bakhtiyar Kaki's itinerary (p. 556) (c. 045).
- Lahore (35): bare departures from Lahore (pp. 118–119) and Kangra "بر سمت شمالی لاهور" (p. 124), "one short bullet at most" (c. 010); punishments, palace, and Lahore only as a road's terminus (p. 136) (c. 011); a bare departure point, "انتهاض[OCR?]" flagged (c. 013); Abu al-Maali's imprisonment (p. 237) and a stay (p. 245), "a consolidator may drop it" (c. 019); campaign material that still says things of the city (c. 020); a halt of "چند روز", "تا لاهور", the revolt "در لاہور" — thin (c. 021); passing campaign mentions (c. 022); mostly Kamran's and Humayun's movements (pp. 286–287, 290, 294), only the council (p. 286) and Kamran's arrival as jagirdar (p. 294) treat the city as a seat (c. 023); itinerary and governance only (c. 024); one item in Bahlul's list (p. 320) and Daulat Khan Lodi's starting point (p. 311), "تخالف[OCR?]" flagged, "a consolidator may drop it" (c. 025); thin: Shaikha Khokhar, an abandoned expedition, the letters (pp. 335–336) (c. 027); "نواحی لاهور", the environs, devastation (c. 030); "بجانب لاهور وبانگر روان شد" (p. 414), year "۴۱۲[OCR?]" flagged (c. 033).
- Peshawar (10): a thin administrative line naming Peshawar's and Kabul's armies (c. 007); a bare camp / march mention (c. 016). Rawalpindi (6): a bare camp / march mention (c. 016). Sialkot (5): a bare office title "فوجدار سیالکوت" — "weigh lightly" (c. 016); Shahi Khan's bare "reached Sialkot" kept as a passing sub-bullet (c. 015). Rohtas: a bare halt (c. 009). Gujrat: a capture and custody on the road (c. 012). Uch: passing (c. 022; second campaign p. 405, c. 032). Siwistan: passing (p. 399, c. 032). Rohtas fort, Khushab and Siwistan get one mention each, and several toponyms are bare locators on a river's course (c. 041). Badin: an extent point only; Shorkot: a boundary only (c. 042).
- **Inconsistent treatment of passing mentions.** c. 015 left its three Lahore mentions unmapped and under Other, "all in passing": Humayun's arrival after defeat (p. 188), Fath Shah dying "بطرف لاهور" (p. 190), the 97-kos distance to Kashmir (p. 184) — "a consolidator may judge the 97-kos road measurement worth a place-level line". c. 004 did not attach Multan for "در زمان بودن ملتان" (p. 51), "a bare time reference". The chunks above mapped comparable passing mentions.

**3. Contents headings only.** c. 047 attached Lahore (35) and Multan (8) place-level on table-of-contents headings alone — "locators to printed pages, not statements of fact": the Dara Shikoh headings (printed ۵۲۱, ۵۲۲, ۵۲۴) and Akbar's march "از لاهور بجانب دکن" (۴۲۵) are campaign passages, and only "عمارات جهانگیرآباد و لاهور" (۴۵۳) concerns the city itself; the body at those printed pages lies outside the chunk. c. 048 did NOT attach the Lahore rows for the ToC heading "صوبه دارالسلطنت لاهور" (p. 594) or the editor's "صوبه لاہور" (p. 598) — "both mean the Mughal suba, not the city" — and treated "صوبه دارالامان ملتان" and "صوبه تهته" (p. 594) as locators; the Langah ToC lines (p. 592; first locator "۲۹۲[OCR?]") name "لنگاه" but not Multan, so nothing was attached.

**4. "شیخ رکن الدین" → shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam by name only.** c. 027: "شیخ الاسلام شیخ رکن الدین" at Multan; NOT taken as Bahauddin Zakariya despite the brief's note that "شیخ الاسلام" at Multan can mean him, because the book names Rukn al-Din explicitly. c. 028: "شیخ رکن الدین ملتانی" matches the row's principal figure "Rukn-ud-Din"; no "رکن عالم", no tomb, no date or lineage; the word before "نزد خود طلبیده" (p. 357) is `[illegible]`, so whether the summons was hostile or honorific is unclear. c. 029: "شیخ رکن الدین" / "شیخ رکن الدین ملتانی", on the name and the Multan setting against the row's "Rukn-ud-Din Abul Fath"; not Jalal al-Din's son the prince "رکن الدین" (p. 368), who takes no id. c. 042: "شیخ رکن الدین" in the Multan list; the p. 527 form "شیخ رکن الدین ابو الفتح سهروردی" matches the row's "Abul Fath"; the p. 528 form, "son of Sadr al-Din Arif, buried in Multan", is not explicitly equated with it by the book. The book never writes "رکن عالم". Left unmapped: the index's "رکن الدین" ("۶۱ - ۶۲") and "رکن الدین شیخ" — several Rukn al-Din headwords, which the index itself distinguishes (c. 003). "خان عالم" and "عالم" are not Rukn-e-Alam (c. 011).

**5. Bare-name person mappings; a deity or saint at a non-archive site mapped in some chunks and not others.** The protocol's many-row rule speaks of passages "where the book names no particular site".
- **Krishna (2 rows).** Mapped: "سری کرشن" only as the subject of the Bhagavata, no site (p. 473), "the consolidator may judge this too thin" (c. 037); two passages naming no site (pp. 479, 481), while the one that places him — the Pandavas weeping for him at Dwarka (p. 479) — was put under Other without ids (c. 038); passages at Indian sites (Dwarka, "سبهالکا" near Somnath), mapped "because the person rule is ruled and is not to be re-litigated. Weigh or drop these at consolidation" (c. 043); the subject of a Persian translation in the source list (p. 583) (c. 047). Not mapped: Krishna not named at Mathura (c. 025); named only at Mathura (p. 416) and Somnath (p. 413), particular non-archive sites (c. 033); "سری کشن" at Mathura (p. 549), "the brief's own example" (c. 044). krishna-mandir-ravi-road sits on the Lahore line only as a Lahore row, not for Krishna (c. 033).
- **"راجه پاندو" → panj-tirath.** Mapped: c. 038 (p. 477), Parikshit's ancestor, in a simile, the row's site (Peshawar) not named — a bare-name person-level mapping under the 18 September person rule; c. 040, the Mahabharata story of Pandu with nothing about Peshawar (grandfather "بچهتر برج[OCR?]" flagged). Rejected wherever the book names only the Pandavas: "زمان پاندوان" dating an umbrella over an idol at "بهون" below Kangra (p. 122) (c. 010); "پاندوان" / "کوران" as chaupar players (p. 381) (c. 030); "نسل پاندوان" (c. 036); "پاندوان" (pp. 465, 470) (c. 037); the Pandavas throughout, "mapping by patronymic would be enrichment from general knowledge", and every place they are put in (Hastinapur, Indrapat, Barnawa, Kampila, Virata, Kurukshetra) is a particular non-archive site — "the consolidator may wish to note that a 'Pandavas' chunk exists, but there is nothing for the row" (c. 039); "پاندوان" (p. 552), and "پاندوگڈه" (p. 546) is a place, Allahabad's southern limit (c. 044); "پاندوان و کوروان" (p. 583) and "راجه جدهشتر پاندوان" (p. 581) (c. 047). The many "پاندوان" of c. 038 are his sons and are not mapped.
- **Aibak → tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak.** Mapped person-level: a bare index name (c. 002); named only as Bakhtiyar's master, also present in the Lahore line by toponym (c. 016); person rule, with no place of death, no burial, no tomb — Lahore named for his accession (p. 401) and his quarrel with Yildiz (p. 400), not his death (c. 032); a bare name in the Delhi fort sequence (p. 561), nothing about the Lahore tomb (c. 045). Not mapped: the errata's "بیبک [OCR?] → ایبک" at printed ۱۸۸ / ۲۵ (p. 48), a bare "ایبک" with no "قطب الدین" (c. 004); "سلطان آرام شاه پسر خوانده سلطان قطب الدین | ۱۸۹" (p. 593), a ToC line naming him only as Aram Shah's adoptive father, no "ایبک", no place, and as transcribed no heading of his own (the line before is "سلطان شهاب الدین عرف معزالدین محمد | ۱۸۴") — check the page image for a dropped line (c. 048).
- **Jagannath (1 row).** Mapped person-level by c. 043, whose sites are all in India (Parsotampur in Orissa). c. 014 did NOT map Orissa's "جگناتہ[OCR?]" (p. 176): "a different place" (wave-1 point 1); jagannath-temple-sialkot is on c. 014's Sialkot line only through Sialkot.
- **Shiva (8 rows).** Mapped: c. 040 (every Mahadev passage concerns temples and springs in suba Kashmir, none an archive site; site names flagged "نوسر[OCR?]", "امرنات[OCR?]", "اسمارو[OCR?]", "پاهنال[OCR?]"); c. 043 (a temple dedication and a river attribution at Indian sites, "not biography"). Not mapped: the index's "مہادیو" — deity or person cannot be told (c. 001); the Mahadev temple "در نزدیکی کشمیر", a particular non-archive site (c. 015); "بتخانه مهاکال" in Malwa (p. 399), no deity named (c. 032); Chamba (p. 512), Purmandal[OCR?] in Jammu (p. 516), Achal via "سوام کارتک خلف مهادیو" (p. 519), under the named-site branch — "flagged in case the consolidator reads the many-row rule more widely"; the Shivrat festival at Tilla (p. 514) is recorded under tilla-jogian (c. 041); "مهادیو" at Baijnath (p. 544), Nimkhar (p. 545) and Banaras (p. 547) (c. 044). Hardwar names no deity (c. 026).
- **Durga.** churrio-jabal-durga-mata-temple person-level for the Durga passages in suba Kashmir (c. 040), and for "درگا", named only as Hinglaj's deity (c. 042). "بهوانی" (Nagarkot p. 518; Trikuta p. 512) is not equated with any archive figure (Durga or Kali) and is not mapped (c. 041).
- **Rama.** ram-mandir-saidpur-ram-kund-mandir mapped for "راجه رام چند", whom the book does not identify further (c. 042), and for Rama named only as the subject of a Persian translation (p. 583), single-row person rule (c. 047). NOT mapped: "راجه رام چندر" at Ajodhya (p. 546), applying §9.316 rule 1 "by analogy, as a deity at a particular non-archive site" — "if the consolidator holds that the single-row person rule (18 Sept) prevails, the Ajodhya bullet is the material" (c. 044).
- **Nanak Mata → Guru Nanak's 18 rows (c. 045).** The book names a site, Nanak Mata near Sambhal, 40 kos north of Delhi, that is none of the 18 rows; mapped "because of the person rule and the coordinator's instruction for Delhi-context mentions, and because the fact (devotees' worship) concerns his following" — "the consolidator may prefer to treat it as an 'Other sites' item".
- c. 041 left the same kind of material unmapped (Shiva above; "بهوانی"; Gorakhnath not named, Tilla's figure is "بالناتهه[OCR?] جوگی", flagged, the site name "تله[OCR?]" with variant "بله[OCR?]").

**6. Rohtas.** c. 009 mapped a bare "در حوالی رهتاس[OCR?]" to **gurdwara-choa-sahib** through toponym_map's "rohtas fort" — "رهتاس[OCR?]" and the verb "شد[OCR?]" are flagged, nothing of a fort is said, and Rohtas is placed only by the narrative (the return from Kabul, before the Bihat is crossed); "the consolidator may drop it". c. 010 did not map "قلعه رهتاس" (p. 116), where Murad Bakhsh, his mother and Darab Khan's wife were left during the Bengal and Patna campaign — by the book's own sequence not the Kabul-road Rohtas. c. 014 did not map "رہتاس" (p. 182): the emperor arrived and mounted the elephant; which Rohtas is not said and no fort is mentioned — "left for the consolidator". c. 019 mapped it: this Rohtas lies on the Gakhar border, between the Bahat and the Sindh, and nothing of the gurdwara is said. c. 021 mapped it: the book itself separates the Punjab fort "رهتاس پنجاب" (p. 266) from the Bihar fort of Raja Chintaman (p. 270) and the "توابع رهتاس" of Sahsaram (p. 273), which are NOT mapped; the book never says the fort founded "متصل کوه بالنا[OCR?]" (pp. 268–269) is the "رهتاس پنجاب" of p. 266 — the reading rests on the name and the narrative (Lahore, the Gakhars, the barrier against Kabul); Dina and Jhelum are not named; "ہتاس[OCR?]" (p. 262) is not counted as a sure mention. c. 023 did not map "قلعه روهتاس" (p. 290), listed with Jaunpur, Bihar and Chunar among Sher Khan's conquests. c. 041 mapped Rohtas fort (1) place-level, one mention. The index's "روہتاس" may be the Punjab Rohtas or another, and may mix both; not mapped (c. 003).

**7. Musa Gilani.** c. 006 left "شیخ موسے گیلانی[OCR?]", named only as the man "که از طرف دارا شکوه حکومت ملتان داشت" (p. 66), unmapped person-level — the wave-2 rule says not to guess for governors — so the governor fact enters only the Multan place-level line, which already carries the row; "گیلانی" is flagged. c. 042 mapped the Multan saints' "شیخ موسی گیلانی" (p. 528) to **shrine-of-syed-musa-pak** by name: only "Musa" and Multan match; the book has "شیخ", not Syed, and no "Pak" or "Shaheed". The book does not say they are one man. Index: "موسیٰ گیلانی" (locator ۶۱) not mapped — it shares ۶۱ with "یوسف گردیزی", "میرحسینی" and "وجیہ الدین محمدؒ", check that page (c. 001); "شیخ موسیٰ گیلانی" — ۵۲۳ (p. 26) not mapped (c. 002).

**8. Shah Abu al-Maali — left unmapped against darbar-abul-muali-qadri every time.** The index's "ابوالمعالی شاہ - ۴۳۴ [OCR?] - ۳۵۲ - ۳۵۳" (p. 46), with a separate "ابوالمعالی میرآتش - ۵۳۱" showing the name is shared (c. 004); a "سیدزادہ" courtier killed near Kabul by Mirza Hakim (pp. 236–237), not connected to any saint or tomb in Lahore (c. 019); "شاه ابوالمعالی" (p. 255), a commander given an army and the management of Punjab affairs, faulted for mistreating his troops, present only through the Lahore line (c. 020); the ToC's "دربیان کشته شدن شاه ابو المعالی | ۳۵۲" (p. 592), in Akbar's reign, a killing, no title, nisba or place — "weigh it against the body text at printed ۳۵۲" (c. 048).

**9. Multan as province or as city.** Filed as PROVINCE punjab, not on the 8 Multan rows: "ولایت ملتان" (p. 298), following c. 042, though "a consolidator may judge it to be really about Multan itself (ruling (c)(4))" (c. 024); "ولایت تهتهه[OCR?] و ملتان و پنجاب" (p. 479), a mythic journey, under punjab (+ sindh) — "a consolidator who reads 'ملتان' as the city could attach those 8 rows place-level" (c. 038); "ولایت ملتان" (p. 581), the realm of the Multan sultans — "a consolidator may prefer the 8 Multan rows" (c. 047); suba Multan with suba Lahore in the punjab bullet (c. 042). But attached to the Multan CITY line: "ولایت ملتان" (p. 405), a siege or conquest of the town (c. 032); "ولایت" (p. 417) and "ان ملک" (p. 420), following c. 032 (c. 033); the governorships "ولایت لاهور و دیپالپور" (p. 324), "ایالت لاهور و ملتان و دیپالپور" (p. 331), "ان ولایت" (p. 327), kept under the city toponyms (c. 026); the c. 042 Multan city bullet itself takes the text under the heading "صوبه دار الامان ملتان", which calls the place "از شهرهاے پاستان" with a "قلعه خشتین". Other: the Multan border "غرب سو ملتان" may mean suba Multan rather than the city (c. 041); "دیپالپور تابع ملتان" (p. 534) not attached — suba, sarkar or city is not said (c. 043); "ملتان" is sometimes the city ("در ملتان توقف ورزید", "حصار ... ملتان", p. 333) and sometimes a governorship ("فتح پور تابع ملتان", "ایالت ... ملتان", pp. 328, 331) (c. 026).

**10. Guru Arjan beyond the Beas (c. 013).** "گورو ارجن[OCR?]", flagged both times, matched to the principal figure "Guru Arjan Dev" — 3 rows (two Lahore rows; the Panjvi-Chhati Patshahi row at Nankana, shared with Guru Hargobind). The meeting is at "منزل گورو ارجن", reached after the Beas crossing from Batala on the road to Thanesar; no town is named, so the many-row rule was applied — but by the route the place lies among those the brief puts outside Pakistan, so wave-1 point 1 ("a Guru at an Indian town") "may require dropping these ids in Pass 2. Recorded here, not resolved." c. 042 attaches the three Guru Arjan rows for a name in the succession list only.

**11. Further single judgements the brief lists.**
- **Eminabad (3)** rests on the spelling "امن آباد" alone: placed only as a manzil on the year-42 Kashmir road and in the Gujrat account; Guru Nanak not named; "the consolidator may drop this line if the identification is not accepted" (c. 014); also an index headword (c. 004) and "امن اباد" (c. 041).
- **sakhi-sarwar at Dhonkal (c. 041).** "سلطان سرور" is clearly a saint's name, but the place described is Dhonkal[OCR?], 12 kos from Sialkot, not the row's site at Sakhi Sarwar / Dera Ghazi Khan, which the chunk never names — "this material may belong to a separate Dhonkal site". In c. 042 the mapping rests on "سلطان سرور" plus the tomb's location relative to Multan (p. 528); the book never writes "سخی" or "لکهداتا" and never names Nigaha or Dera Ghazi Khan.
- **"Gobind Rai" → gurdwara-dash-mesh-pita (c. 041)** rests on a name-form match the chunk does not make: "گورو گوبند راے" / "گوروگوبند رائے", son of Tegh Bahadur, against the row's "Guru Gobind Singh"; the row's site (Sukkur) is not named — "the consolidator should confirm or drop it". Contrast c. 002: the index's "گوبندراے گورو" (p. 22) was NOT mapped, since it matches no principal figure verbatim and "equating the two names would be general knowledge".
- **c. 042's name-only matches** (person-level, bare names): **darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz**, named only as Lahore's restorer, the index giving the row no city or province; **loh-temple-lava-temple** on "لو"; **ram-mandir-saidpur-ram-kund-mandir** on "راجه رام چند"; **churrio-jabal-durga-mata-temple** on "درگا", Hinglaj's deity; **gurdwara-rori-sahib** on "مردانه", the row's co-principal figure; **gurdwara-guru-ram-das-ji**, the three Guru Arjan rows and the six Guru Hargobind rows, named only in the succession list; **data-darbar** on "میر علی حجویری", not "Data Ganj Bakhsh".
- **Talwandi.** c. 042 took "تلونڈی رائے بهوئه[OCR?]" to the 7 Nankana Sahib rows by the brief's equation Talwandi = Nankana Sahib, which the book does not make; place-level, no gurdwara named. c. 026 did NOT link "تلوندی رائے کمال الدین مبین[OCR?]" (p. 327) or "تلوندی[OCR?] تهاره[OCR?]" (p. 330): the book ties both to a Rai Kamal al-Din and "زمینداران تهاره" in campaign itineraries (across the Sutlej toward Ludhiana; on Iqbal Khan's road to Dipalpur), and nowhere connects them with Nanak or with p. 520's "تلونڈی رائے بهوئه" — the equation was read as applying to Nanak's Talwandi and "flagged for the consolidator rather than applied". The index's "تلونڈی" was not mapped (c. 003).

**Other inconsistencies found in the Doubts**
- **Malik Ayaz.** Mapped to darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz on "ملک ایاز" plus the book's "سرامد" of Sultan Mahmud's slaves — no Lahore, governorship, office, death or tomb, and the row has no province in the index (c. 032); and as Lahore's restorer (c. 042). Not mapped: the index's three Ayaz headwords "ایاز - ۶۴ - ۱۷۹ - ۱۸۱" (p. 43), "احمد ایاز - ۲۵۴" and "اعزالدین ایاز - ۱۹۱" (pp. 44–45), none identified — "a consolidator could check the body at those printed pages" (c. 004); "احمد ایاز عرف خواجه جهان", "از مقربان سلطان محمدشاه" (p. 344), a name trap (c. 027); "ملک اعزالدین[OCR?] ایاز حاکم ملتان" (p. 398), a different man by the book's chronology, placed only in the Multan bullet (c. 032). No Ayaz in c. 024, 034, 047.
- **Peshawar's old name.** c. 002 refused to read the index's "فرشاور" (p. 24) as Peshawar ("general knowledge"); c. 033 attached the Peshawar rows on the book's own old names "فرشاور", "پرشاور" and "بگرام" and the battle of "۳۹۱" (p. 424).
- **Gujrat and Shah Daula.** shrine-of-hazrat-shah-daula-daryai was attached through toponym_map's "gujrat" from context in c. 012 (the prince on the Chenab at the Shahpur and Sodhra[OCR?] fords, fetched from Lahore, pp. 146–145; "به[OCR?] گجرات" flagged once, p. 145; Shah Daula not named) and c. 014 (land inside Sialkot, after the Chenab crossing, the Gujar zamindari; "بگجرات[OCR?]" flagged; Shah Daula not named), and in c. 041, where the Punjab town in the Chaj doab is named with Shah Daula. c. 005 left "گجرات" (p. 52) unplaced — Dara heads there from Multan, nothing to choose between suba Gujarat and Gujrat in Punjab. c. 047 left two ToC headings undetermined: "دربیان مقرر گردیدن پرگنه گجرات دامن آباد", printed ۴۱۲ (p. 591) — "پرگنه" may point to the Punjab town, check the body at printed ۴۱۲ — and "آمدن داراشکوه از تهته[OCR?] بجانب گجرات", printed ۵۳۸ (p. 589). The index's "گجرات" (p. 22), one headword with a long locator run, does not separate the two and takes no id (c. 002).
- **Siwistan → Sehwan Sharif (2).** Attached by the brief's equation of سیوستان with Sehwan, though toponym_map has no "siwistan" key and Lal Shahbaz is never named: c. 004 (fort, narrow river, "محل بیم و مکان هلاکت"; town or wider district not said; neither Lal Shahbaz nor Lakhi Shah Saddar named; "[illegible]" before "سیوستان", p. 50), c. 032, c. 041 ("ولایت سیوستان" is a region, not the town), c. 042 (a sarkar). c. 014 attached Sehwan on "سهوان" (fort and "ولایت") through toponym_map's "sehwan sharif"; shrine-of-lakhi-shah-saddar only because toponym_map lists it under that key ("near Sehwan Sharif"); no saint, tomb or shrine named; "سهوان" itself is never flagged.
- **Shorkot and Jhang.** garh-maharaja-shorkot attached on the index's "شورکوت" (c. 002), as a boundary (c. 042) and, at district level only, through Jhang (c. 041); "قلعه شور" / "شور" not equated by the book with "شورکوت" and not mapped (p. 296, c. 023; pp. 325–326, c. 026 — "a consolidator may weigh this").
- **Guru Hargobind and Guru Ram Das.** Index and errata not mapped: "رامداس", one of whose locators "۳۷۷" may be another man; "چک گوز و ہزگوبند[OCR?]", garbled, a Hargobind reading unsupported (c. 003); "ہرگوبیند → ہرگوبند" at printed ۷۰ / ۴ (p. 48), a bare spelling correction, though the index places "بابا نانک" and "امرداس" at printed ۶۹–۷۰ (c. 004). Mapped: Hargobind's 6 rows person-level, the only place named for him Kiratpur, no row (p. 513), and the 37-year incumbent's name falls on p. 520 — the chunk never says it was Hargobind; only the following clause connects Har Rai's succession to "جد بزرگوار" and to Hargobind (c. 041); the succession list (c. 042). "چک گورو هرگوبند" (p. 523), a Bari-doab place carrying the Guru's name, is not attached (c. 042).
- **Bahauddin Zakariya.** Person-level as an ancestor or father only: Shaikh Yusuf's ancestor (p. 297; everything else in the bullet concerns the descendant) (c. 023); a bare ancestor mention, Sadr al-Din having no row (c. 027); Sadr al-Din's father, with epithets and "قدس الله سره", not placed at Multan, no tomb, one epithet word "[illegible]" ("اولیاے [illegible] اصفیاے") (c. 031); "شیخ بهار الدین ذکریا", printed with "بهار" and no flag, read as Baha al-Din on "ذکریا" with "ملتان" in the clause (p. 556), nothing said of the shrine (c. 045). Not chosen: the unnamed "مزارات مشایخ" visited in 835[OCR?] (c. 026). Index headword mapped (c. 004).
- **Farid.** Person-level on the index's bare name (c. 002) and at "قصبه اجودهن" as a living shaikh (p. 556) — Ajodhan is not equated with Pakpattan in that chunk and his tomb is not named, so it is not treated as a Pakpattan toponym (c. 045). Not mapped: the index's "شیخ فرید" (p. 26), "فرید بخاری" (p. 24) (c. 002) and "اجودھن - ۳۳ - ۶۲" (p. 46) (c. 004).

### Archive mappings and declines

*Every hedge and every decline Pass 1 recorded (cross-checked against `ext/hedges.md`), grouped by row or place. Items already given in full above are only pointed to.*

**Place-level toponym lines (ruling (a)) — what each rests on, and what was not attached**
- **Lahore (35).** Place-level throughout; the basis per chunk is in item 2 above. Lahore fort material stays on the Lahore line, which already carries both "lahore fort" rows: "دروازه هیتبه[OCR?] پول[OCR?] قلعه مبارک" (c. 006); "قلعه ارک" (p. 107), not named as Lahore's fort in so many words but set at Lahore (c. 009); Khusrau's prison "در قلعه" (p. 145), Lahore's by the run of the narrative (c. 012); "قلعه" at Lahore (pp. 325, 327), not otherwise identified (c. 026); Akbar's "قلعه" and "دولتخانه" (c. 042). The dawlatkhana "در دارالسلطنت لاهور" under the heading "عمارات ... لاهور" is kept on the line; "the consolidator may move it under 'Other' by the named-building rule"; it is not said to be in the fort (c. 011). The other "دولتخانه قلعه مبارک", entered on 4 Rabi al-Awwal (p. 64), is at the capital, not Lahore (c. 006). Not attached: "باغ فیض بخش المشهور بشالامار" (c. 006) and "باغ شالامار" (c. 042) — toponym_map lists "shalimar gardens" for **madho-lal-hussain**, but that row is only "near" the garden and the book says nothing of Shah Husain (also c. 041); Jahangir's tomb and "باغ مهدی[OCR?] قاسم خان" at "شاه دره", a named tomb and garden with no "shahdara" key in toponym_map ("فقندسی[OCR?]" unclear, "[holy?]" tentative; "نقش[OCR?]" read "نعش" only tentatively; "چکر[OCR?] هستی[OCR?]" not identified) (c. 009); "باغ کامران میرزا", a named garden (c. 012); Hujwiri's tomb, not treated as the toponym "data darbar", which would bring in **peer-makki** and darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz, because the book does not use that place-name (c. 042); the editor's remark on the "Panjab" and Lahore (p. 14), which evaluates the book (c. 001); "دروازه لاهوری" (p. 505), a gate of Kabul (c. 040); "قصبه سلطان پور" on the Beas, not treated as Lahore (c. 031); suba Lahore in the ToC and the editor (c. 048). "از لاهور" (p. 399), in "وسلطان بالشکر بسیار از لاهور رسیده مقابله نمود", is quoted as printed: whether Iltutmish came from Lahore or to it is not settled; the p. 400/399 join is read "از استماع آمدن / ملک از لاهور درحوالی دهلی ...", Aram Shah moving from Lahore toward Delhi (c. 032). "ولایت لاهور" (p. 404) is on the city line (c. 032). The governor's name before "صوبه دار لاهور" (p. 62) falls across the page break after "خلیل الله خان [illegible]" (p. 63); c. 006 records Khalilullah Khan's appointment to Lahore (p. 64), but c. 005's text does not itself join name to title (c. 005).
- **Multan (8).** Basis per chunk in item 2; province-or-city in item 9. prahladpuri-temple is on the line through toponym_map's "fort of multan" for the coup "درون قلعه" (p. 296) (c. 023) and Bahauddin's tomb "درون قلعه" (c. 042); the book says nothing of a temple. The "مزارات مشایخ" visited in 835[OCR?] are unnamed, so no single row is chosen, and "بملتان رسیده مزارات مشایخ را طواف نموده" places them at Multan only by sequence (c. 026). The governor Musa Gilani enters only this line (c. 006); so does "ملک اعزالدین[OCR?] ایاز حاکم ملتان" (p. 398) (c. 032). "از فتح پور تابع ملتان که دارالایالت او بود" (p. 328): the "دارالایالت" may be Fatehpur or Multan (c. 026). Not attached: "در زمان بودن ملتان" (p. 51) (c. 004); "ولایت ... ملتان و پنجاب" (p. 479) (c. 038); "دیپالپور تابع ملتان" (p. 534) (c. 043); the Langah ToC lines (p. 592) (c. 048). "دراد[OCR?]" in "که دراد[OCR?] مغل است" is flagged, its sense ("in the path of"?) unsettled (c. 029). "چهجو" is flagged (c. 030). "غورک[OCR?]" is not located (c. 033).
- **Uch (5).** Index headword (c. 004); passing, "اوچ تابع ملتان", with "و را[OCR?] و اوچ" garbled and "by way of Uch" tentative (c. 022); Pir Muhammad took its "حصار" (p. 333) (c. 026); its conquest from "هتاهته[OCR?]" (p. 405) (c. 032); c. 042. Held pair: above.
- **Peshawar (10).** Index headword, flagged (c. 004); thin administrative (c. 007); bare camp / march (c. 016); the old names "فرشاور", "پرشاور", "بگرام" and the battle of "۳۹۱" (p. 424), with the editor's footnote line on p. 424 "[illegible]" under the eLibrary stamp — its only visible fragment "(۱) ... شاور [OCR?]" stands against marker (۱), though the note on Peshawar is marker (۴), so which note it belongs to is not settled (c. 033); a general description of the city, its roads and the "بنگرام[OCR?]" tuman, naming none of the ten rows' sites; the Khyber-road station spelled "دهیکه[OCR?]" and "دکه[OCR?]" (p. 501), not equated (c. 040). Index "فرشاور" not read as Peshawar (c. 002).
- **Sialkot (5).** Sialkot as an administrative unit ("داخل سیالکوت", "تابع سیالکوت"), not the town; jagannath-temple-sialkot on the line only through Sialkot (c. 014). The Maulana Kamal material — a scholar-dervish who settled and taught there, "the only thing the book says about life at Sialkot"; "مشهور [illegible] الاکناف" not filled; "هندوستان[OCR?]" and "بفضایل[OCR?]" flagged; Maulana Kamal not identified further, no row has him as principal figure; the book names none of the rows (c. 015). A bare office title (c. 016). Its seizure and the order to lay it waste and resettle it; **allo-mahar** is on the line because toponym_map lists it under "sialkot", but its index location is the village of Allo Mahar while the book means the town (c. 024). The ancient fort rebuilt and besieged (p. 404) (c. 032). c. 041, c. 042. Left without ids in the index (c. 003).
- **Sukkur (5).** Its distance, the forest and thorn near it, the Qandahar road branching 25 kos away, its faujdar; no shrine; gurdwara-pehli-patshahi-jind-pir-sukkur is also a Guru Nanak row (c. 004). Bhakkar is not mapped to the Sukkur or Rohri rows, since c. 014 names neither place.
- **Rawalpindi (6).** A bare camp / march mention (c. 016); the gap "[illegible] حضور [illegible]" in that sentence (p. 200) is not filled.
- **Sehwan Sharif (2).** See "Siwistan" above; c. 014's Sehwan events in Contradictions.
- **Amarkot (2).** Index (c. 004); "امرکوت" attached by the index's own row name "Umarkot (Amarkot) Shiv Mandir" and toponym_map's "umarkot" / "umerkot"; shrine-of-makhdoom-abdul-rahim-girhori lies only "near Pithoro, Umarkot", so its attachment is weaker still; the book says nothing of the temple or the saint (c. 022); "امرکوت" is a sarkar (c. 042).
- **Ichhra (2).** Index (c. 004). **shah-jamal** and **shrine-of-baba-shah-kamal** by toponym_map's "ichhra", from a passing camp mention; the index places shrine-of-baba-shah-kamal only "near Ichhra"; "اچهره" flagged at every occurrence; placed "ان طرف لاهور" (p. 67) and "نزدیکی لاهور" (p. 65) (c. 006).
- **Khushab.** **amb-temples-amb-sharif** by toponym_map's "khushab": the book's "خوشاب" is a faujdari named with Bhera, not further located; nothing is said of the temple complex (c. 006). Jhang and Khushab match the rows only at district level: garh-maharaja-shorkot is at Garh Maharaja (Shorkot) and amb-temples-amb-sharif on Sakesar (c. 041).
- **Hasan Abdal → gurdwara-panja-sahib.** A halt, two burials and a garden; neither Nanak nor the Panja is named; the two tombs and the garden are not rows (c. 014). The coordinator flagged Hasan Abdal for c. 015, but "حسن ابدال" does not occur on pp. 183–195 as transcribed; nothing mapped.
- **Jhelum (2), Salt Range (1), Rohtas fort (1), Jhang (1), Khushab (1), Gujrat (1), Eminabad (3)** in c. 041, all place-level; the Salt Range row via the book's "همین کوه" link from Tilla. "آب جهلم" (p. 326) is the river, so the toponym "jhelum" (gurdwara-choa-sahib, tilla-jogian) is not attached (c. 026).
- **Badin (1).** Index (c. 004); **dargah-of-khwaja-muhammad-zaman-luari-sharif** as an extent point only (c. 042).
- **Nankana Sahib (7).** See Talwandi, item 11.
- **Gujrat (1).** See "Gujrat and Shah Daula" above. In c. 041 the Gujrat town bullet carries the same single id as the Shah Daula bullet, place-level.

**Person- and site-level attachments: caveats**
- **Guru Nanak (18 rows)**, person-level everywhere: bare index headwords (c. 001, 004); only inside Guru Har Rai's title, no site, Har Rai no row, "کشیر[OCR?]" read "کثیر" (large) tentatively (c. 006); founder of the sajjada and author of Hindi verses, "near a bare name" — and "که در معرفت الهی مشهور بود" follows "بابا نانک" directly, so it may describe Nanak or Guru Arjan, not resolved (c. 013); "بابا نانک", his followers and their practices (p. 519), no Nanak site (c. 041); "مکان بابا نانک", twelve kos from Batala on the Ravi, and the unnamed "دهی از دیهات بتاله" not identified with any row, so no Kartarpur attachment (c. 042); Nanak Mata (c. 045, item 5); the object of the Udasi's belief — the passage does not itself say "مقتدای خویش" and "مرشد خویش" are Baba Nanak, "though that is the natural reading" (c. 046). No Guru is named in c. 012 ("گوندوال[OCR?]", p. 146, is only the battle site with Khusrau) or c. 011.
- **Guru Arjan (3), Guru Hargobind (6), Guru Ram Das, Gobind Rai, Mardana** — items 10, 11 and "Guru Hargobind and Guru Ram Das" above. "تیغ بہادر" is not the principal figure of any row (c. 003).
- **data-darbar** — c. 001 and c. 042 (above).
- **shrine-of-shah-yusaf-gardez** — c. 001 (above). "شیخ یوسف قریشی" is NOT mapped to `shrine-of-shah-yusaf-gardez` or `shah-yousuf`: the book calls him "قریشی", a descendant of Bahauddin, and gives him no tomb (c. 023). "مهتر یوسف علیه السلام" (p. 502) is the prophet Joseph, not shah-yousuf or shrine-of-shah-yusaf-gardez (c. 040).
- **shrine-of-imam-ali-ul-haq** (c. 041): "خلف امام زین العابدین" is printed as the book gives it; the account is framed "گویند" (it is said); "بسیالکوت[OCR?]" is flagged; the verb "یافتند" is plural — the martyrdom is the whole band's. The index's "علی الحق[OCR?]" — ۷۳ (p. 25) could be the row's "Imam Ali-ul-Haq", but is flagged, has no "امام" and names no Sialkot; held for a body chunk; ۷۳ is also given for "ظفروال", "عبدالحکیم" and "صفدر خان" (p. 26) (c. 002). Imam Ali-ul-Haq is not named in c. 015 or c. 016.
- **shrine-of-hazrat-shah-daula-daryai** (c. 041): flagged readings "هفدهم[OCR?]" (regnal year of death), "کهابدهره[OCR?]" (his first master), "باب الله[OCR?]" and "میان سیدنا(۱)" (variant "سیدنام[OCR?]"); "مزاران بزرگوار" is unflagged in print, Pass 1's reading "مزار آن بزرگوار" offered in brackets; the book spells the name "شاه دولا". "شاه دره" is not Shah Daula (c. 009); "شاه شمس الدین دریائی" at Dipaliwal is neither this row nor the Multan "Shams" (c. 042).
- **sakhi-sarwar** — item 11. "غازیخان" (p. 296) is a person, not Dera Ghazi Khan; "سخی[OCR?]" (p. 293) is an adjective for Khwaja Mahdi (c. 023).
- **shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam**, **shrine-of-syed-musa-pak**, the Uch pair, the "Shams" row, **langer-makhdoom**, **tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak**, **darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz**, **panj-tirath**, **shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya**, **shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar** — above.
- **gorakhnath-goraknath-temple** is mapped through the row's location_short ("Gor Khatri area, Peshawar"), not its principal figure: the book names "کورکهتری" as a "معبد جوگیان" (jogis' temple) demolished under Shah Jahan and never names Gorakhnath; whether the demolished temple is the row's temple is for the consolidator (c. 040). Not mapped for the general jogis of c. 046.
- **"امروز تیج[OCR?] تیرتهه" (p. 503)** is not read: the book does not make clear whether this "today" clause describes the Kor Khatri site or a different place by a spring-fed tank; the first word is flagged and unrepaired; because a Peshawar row is named Panj Tirath, the reading matters to **panj-tirath** — a reviewer should check the lithograph. No site-level mapping was made on its strength; panj-tirath carries the passage only through the Peshawar bullet (c. 040). In the same chunk "زنان خوردش" (p. 498) is printed in the plural after the book has named only two wives; not resolved.
- **The 8 Shiva rows and churrio-jabal-durga-mata-temple** (c. 040): editor's footnotes (۱) on "پاهنال[OCR?]" and (۲) on "پیتلهام[OCR?]" are illegible ("covered by the source stamp", p. 508).
- **tilla-jogian** carries the Shivrat festival at Tilla (p. 514) (c. 041).

**Declines with no mapping anywhere (or none for that passage)**
- **sharada-peeth** never attached, because Sharada is never named: "سری نگر", the hill country reached from the Ganga with its own raja ("مرزبان سری نگر"), not equated with Kashmir (c. 006); Kashmir the valley (c. 009, 010, 014, 015, 041); "کشمیر" (p. 286), Mirza Haidar's conquest (c. 023); "مرزبان کشمیر" (c. 026); "والی کشمیر", Ayaz's father's realm (c. 032); hills, a "دره" and the fort "لوه کوت" (pp. 420, 416, 414) (c. 033); the Durga temple "اسمارو[OCR?]" on the river "پدمنی[OCR?]" (p. 507) in suba Kashmir — "a reviewer should check the lithograph before anything is attached to the AJK row" (c. 040); Kashmir in the list of lands Delhi's residents come from (p. 559) (c. 045); "سری نگر" (p. 552) on the Ganga between Badrinath and "رکهی کیش" (c. 044).
- **tilla-jogian / gorakhnath-goraknath-temple / Gorakhnath:** the jogis of p. 133 are at no named site (c. 011); "درویشان" (p. 98) are Hindu jogis and sannyasis in general, no deity named (c. 008); "سلسلهٔ جوگی" (p. 440) is a royal line, not the Nath yogis (c. 034); "جوگی" / "جوگیان" (pp. 443–442) name no site or figure (c. 035); "جوگیان" in general (p. 567) (c. 046); "گورکهپور" (p. 545) is a sarkar (c. 044); no Goraknath in c. 038.
- **katas-raj-temples** NOT mapped: the Kahyala pool (p. 514), regarded as an ancient temple where Hindus bathe at the sun's entry into Aries and as the earth's "left eye", has its name damaged — "کهنتا[OCR?] چهه[OCR?] کولابی[OCR?]", the editor's variant for "کهیاله" being "کهتاز[OCR?]"; the chunk names neither Katas nor Choa Saidanshah; the material sits under the Salt Range bullet (c. 041).
- not mapped, `mausoleum-of-waris-shah`: Waris Shah is not named in the Hir–Ranjha passages (c. 041).
- **madho-lal-hussain** via Shalamar: declined (c. 006, 041, 042).
- **Mansehra rows**: Pakhli and Dhantur[OCR?], on the Indus / Bihat course, have no toponym_map key (c. 041).
- **rahman-baba-mausoleum-rehman-baba-shrine**: "مهمند" (pp. 504, 502) is a tribe (c. 040).
- not mapped, `shrine-of-pir-lakha-aab-e-shifa-jhal-magsi`: "لاکها(۲)" is a courtesan's name (c. 036).
- **shrine-of-lakhi-shah-saddar**: the index's bare "صدرالدین" (p. 26) (c. 002); "لکهی جنگل" in sarkar Dipalpur, placed on the Beas and Sutlej, is not the toponym "lakhi" (c. 042).
- **shrine-of-baba-shah-kamal**: the index's "کمال شیخ" (p. 23) (c. 002); "مولانا ئے کمال", treated as a name because the book gives it as his name (c. 015).
- **shah-yousuf**: "گذر شاه پور" on the Chenab (p. 146) — toponym_map's "shahpur" points to shah-yousuf ("Shah Yousuf, Shahpur, Punjab"), but the book names only a ford where no boat was found; "the consolidator should weigh it" (c. 012); "قصبه شاهپور تابع قصبه نورپور", on the Ravi in the hills (c. 041).
- **The Okara "shamsabad" key**: the index's "شمس آباد" (p. 27), common name, nothing ties it there (c. 002); the book's "شمس آباد" on the Bihat between Nandana and Bhera, at the foot of the salt hills (c. 041).
- not mapped, `sain-vali-vilayat-rai-darbar-kambar`: "موضع کمبر[OCR?]" (p. 212) is flagged, unlocated, in court marvels (c. 017).
- **Hinglaj**: the index's "ہنکلاخ[OCR?]" (locator ۶۰) is not "هنگلاج" and was not placed (c. 001). The book places Hinglaj in its suba Thatta section, seventy kos from Thatta, but the row's province tag is balochistan; the sindh province bullet is not meant to reach it (c. 042).
- **Udasi rows** ("Nanakpanthi / Udasi Darbar", mostly in Sindh) are not mapped by category; "the consolidator may wish to note the general Udasi passage (p. 566) for those rows". The Jati and Sarpura are not mapped (c. 046).
- **shrine-of-hafiz-muhammad-jamal-multani** (Hafiz Muhammad Jamal Multani): "دیوان خواجه حافظ" (p. 126) is a book produced in a juggler's trick (c. 010).
- **shah-jamal**: "بخاری" in Farid Bukhari's name is not taken to any Bukhari row (Mauj Darya, Jalaluddin Surkh-Posh, Shah Jamal) (c. 012); "جمال خان حاکم جونپور" (c. 021).
- **"هزاره"**, a place on the Chenab, is not the Hazara region (c. 041). "ہشتگان[OCR?]" is not the "hashtnagri" of Peshawar; the river "بہت" is not the town of Jhelum (c. 019).
- **Ajmer**: "روضه رضویه[OCR?]" — the saint is not named, the adjective flagged, Ajmer outside scope (c. 016).

**Places with no row or toponym_map key (not attached, not placed)**
- **Thatta** has no row in toponym_map (c. 004, 009, 011, 013, 014, 021, 022, 027, 032, 038, 041, 042): only "صوبه داری تهتهه" (c. 004) and "صوبه داری تهتهه[OCR?]" (p. 105) (c. 009) were taken as PROVINCE sindh; town and campaign material went under Other — the siege of Thatta's fort (p. 115) and Mahabat's march (pp. 109–110) by ruling (c)(4) (c. 009); bare "تهته[OCR?]", grouped with faujdars, NOT taken to sindh (c. 007); "بصوب تهتهه" (p. 116), toward the town (c. 010); "فتح تهته" (p. 142), no "صوبه" — "the consolidator may weigh this" (c. 011); "تهتهه[OCR?]" (p. 161), a single death-place mention (c. 013); town and campaign under Other, the country under sindh (c. 014); "تهته" (p. 269) in "ملتان و تهته" (c. 021); "تہتہ", Shah Husain Arghun's realm, not called a suba (c. 022); "حاکم تهته" and "از تهته" may mean the city (c. 023); "تهته[OCR?]", "تهتهه" (pp. 340, 345), a town, its Jam and "ان دیار" (c. 027); Thatta / Dewal (c. 032). Not Thatta: "تهتهه[OCR?]" (p. 108), a stage on the return from Kashmir between "بهیرم کله[OCR?]" and "راجوری" (c. 009); "تهتهه[OCR?] تابع ملتان", a dependency of Multan, not identified further (c. 018); "تهته[OCR?]" in "شیر خان تهته[OCR?] تعلقه گکهران رفته" (p. 269), meaning unclear (c. 021); "بهوچ پور[OCR?] تهته[OCR?] کنار دریای گنگ" (p. 289), on the Ganges (c. 023); "تتهه[OCR?]", conquered by the Kashmir ruler (p. 327), cannot be confirmed as Thatta (c. 026); "تهنه[OCR?]" (p. 421) and "تهتهه[OCR?]" (p. 420), not equated by the chunk (c. 033).
- **Bhakkar** ("بهکر" / "بهکهر") has no toponym_map row and is not taken to sindh (c. 004, 006, 014 — its sieges under Other, 020 — "بهکر و تته" (p. 253) a bare list entry, 022, 041, 042); "ولایت بهکر" (printed ۴۱۸) gives no suba (c. 047). "بهکهر" / "بهکهر[OCR?]", the Khokhars' seat (pp. 326–327), is not "بهکر" (c. 026). "بهکر[OCR?]" (p. 535) is a lake three kos from Ajmer, no reading offered (c. 043).
- **Dipalpur** has no row (c. 024, 025 — pp. 318, 320, 322, 028); "موضع دیپالپور" / "دیپال پور" (p. 95) is a Malwa village between the Narmada and Ujjain (c. 008); "دیالپور[OCR?]" (p. 327), paired with Lahore, may be Dipalpur (c. 026); the chunk never mentions Ajodhan or Patan, so nothing goes to the Farid row (c. 026); the book makes "سرکار دیپالپور" part of suba Multan (pp. 525–526) (c. 042).
- **Other places with no row:** "قلعه اتک بنارس" (Attock), in no suba — "بنارس" part of the fort's name, "بنارسی" the runner's name; "ولایت بهار" (p. 115), unflagged, not interpreted (c. 009); "ششینو[OCR?] پور", "ساهوملی[OCR?]" and Jahangirabad, said only to be "در پنجاب" (pp. 136–135), and "دهود[OCR?]" (p. 137) (c. 011); "منزل علی مسجد" (p. 144), Peshawar not named; Sultanpur, Goindwal[OCR?], Fathabad[OCR?]/Nahrwal[OCR?], Sodhra[OCR?]; "شاه لالان[OCR?]" (p. 144), the popular name of two Kabul gardens (c. 012); Achal ("مه[OCR?] مکان اچل[OCR?]"; "سناسیان[OCR?]" flagged), Batala, Thanesar[OCR?], the Deccan places, Allahabad, "پتنه[OCR?]"; Bangash ("بنگش[OCR?]", p. 166), unplaced, no KP province; the hill places on p. 169, unlocated (c. 013); Pakhli[OCR?], Damtur[OCR?], Dakka[OCR?], Bhimbar[OCR?] ("بہنبر"), Kunjah, the tappa "الوور[OCR?] اٹیچ[OCR?]" (c. 014); Hansi (p. 402) is Rai Pithora's fort, no saint connected (c. 032); Hansi has no row (c. 045). "پرسرور [OCR?]" (p. 39) is not Sakhi Sarwar, and "پتن [OCR?]" (p. 39) is not taken as Pakpattan (c. 004).
